     mera.health take-home: plan draft (iteration 1)

     Context

     Meenakshi (hospital ops) asks: "which diabetic patients are overdue for
     follow-up so the front desk can call them". The data is four messy CSVs.
     The brief grades process: defining "diabetic" and "overdue", care with
     health data, and questions that change what we build. Deliverables are
     PLAN.md (committed before any code, half a page, never edited afterwards),
     code that runs in one or two commands, DECISIONS.md (2 pages max),
     reply.txt (200 words max, non-technical), real git history, and agent
     transcripts. The time budget is about 4 hours.

     This file is our working draft. Once you sign off, section 3 becomes
     PLAN.md, verbatim.

     ---

     0. Two housekeeping issues to fix first

     1. The git repo is wrong. The take-home folder sits inside a git repo
        rooted at C:\Users\Abhay JK (your whole home directory, branch master).
        Committing there would be a disaster. Run a fresh git init inside
        engineering-intern-take-home/ (it gets its own .git, which shadows the
        parent repo) before the first commit.
     2. Downloads/CLAUDE.md belongs to an unrelated project (VLM ID-forgery). It
        gets auto-loaded because it sits in a parent folder. I'm ignoring it.
        You may want to move it so it doesn't leak into transcripts and confuse
        reviewers.

     1. What a first read of the data shows (read-only, no code yet)

     Dataset: 300 patients; about 815 visits from Oct 2023 to Sep 2026; about
     425 lab rows; about 540 prescription rows.

     There is no diagnosis column. "Diabetic" has to be inferred from three
     signals, and each one is noisy:
     - Visit notes (free text): "T2DM", "DM2", "k/c/o DM", "Known diabetic",
       "NIDDM", "Juvenile diabetes", "T1DM". The notes also contain the traps:
       - Gestational diabetes: SVH027966, SVH028068, SVH028326. This resolves
         after delivery. But SVH027966 later has HbA1c 6.6%.
       - Prediabetes / IGT / "borderline": SVH024742, SVH025926, SVH028864,
         SVH029638, SVH029659.
       - Type 1 children under Paediatrics: SVH025870, SVH028258, SVH029838. The
         person to call is a parent.
     - Prescriptions: Metformin is also given for PCOS (SVH025191, SVH028876,
       SVH030554) and for GDM. So "on metformin" ≠ diabetic. Drug names are
       inconsistent: Tab./T./TAB/Inj., and combination products.
     - Labs: The same test appears under different names and units:
       - HbA1c is recorded as A1C, HbA1c and Glycated Hb; the last is in
         mmol/mol, not %.
       - Fasting glucose is recorded as FBS, Glucose (F) and Fasting Glucose;
         the last is in mmol/L.
       - Some HbA1c rows have no unit, e.g. "65" (mmol/mol, about 8.1%).
       - Three date formats are mixed: 2 Oct 23, 03/04/2024 (DD/MM, confirmed by
         row order) and ISO.
       - Two dates are in the future (19/12/2026, 27/02/2027).
       - Some rows are exact duplicates.
       - Some patients have diabetic-range HbA1c but no diabetes note seen yet
         (e.g. SVH025473, SVH027596). They may be undiagnosed, which is a
         doctor's call, not ours.

     People who must not be called:
     - Died: SVH025339, SVH026086, SVH025698. This is recorded only in visit
       note text.
     - Transferred care: SVH024860, SVH025512, SVH028346.

     Identity problems (wrong-person-on-call-list risk):
     - Same person under two MRNs: same DOB and phone, name variants.
       - SVH027863/SVH029322 (Joseph Dsouza/D'Souza)
       - SVH027925/SVH029107 (Shivakumar)
       - SVH025473/SVH029873 (Md./Mohammed Rafiq)
       - SVH027082/SVH030772 (Padmavathi/Padma Reddy)

       If the records aren't merged, the "last visit" date can be wrong and the
       person can appear twice.
     - Same name, different people (must NOT merge): Lakshmi Devi ×2, Ravi Kumar
       ×2, Sunitha Rao ×2.
     - Shared phone numbers across different people (families): 9000090725,
       9000032917, 9000043929. The front desk must ask for the named patient.
     - Missing phone: about 8 patients.
     - Impossible DOB: SVH027832 was born 2025-08-20, registered 2024-06-25, yet
       attends Diabetology.

     "Overdue" signals:
     - next_appointment is usually blank.
     - Notes often state an interval instead: "review 3 months", "r/v 6/12",
       "Review 6 wks".
     - The export ends about 2026-09-29. Today is 2026-10-08, so the "as-of"
       date matters.

     ---

     2. Draft email to the hospital (max 5 questions)

     Each question changes what gets built. I picked them over the alternatives
     listed below.

     1. Who should count as diabetic? Should the list include only patients a
        doctor has recorded as diabetic? We can also flag patients whose blood
        tests are in the diabetic range but have no diagnosis recorded; those
        would go to a doctor to review, not to the call list. Should Type 1
        children, past gestational diabetes and "prediabetes" be on the list?
     2. What does "overdue" mean to your doctors? Is it (a) past the review date
        the doctor wrote at the last visit, or (b) past a fixed interval, e.g.
        3 months, since the last diabetes check? How many days late before the
        front desk should call?
     3. What counts as a follow-up? Does any visit reset the clock, e.g. a
        dental or skin visit? Or only a visit where diabetes was reviewed? Does
        a blood test alone count?
     4. Is the export complete? What date range and which departments does it
        cover? Are appointments that are already booked for the future in it? If
        not, we may list patients who already have a booking.
     5. How will the front desk use it? Is this a one-time list or a weekly
        re-run on fresh exports? Do they want it printed or in a spreadsheet?
        Should call outcomes be recorded?

     Deliberately NOT asking. We'll work these out ourselves and document them:
     - lab name and unit mapping and date formats
     - diagnostic thresholds (look up ADA/WHO)
     - drug classes
     - how to detect duplicate patients
     - whether deceased or transferred patients should be excluded (obviously
       yes)

     Alternative Q5: "Can someone confirm the suspected duplicate
     registrations?" It's arguably more data-quality focused. I currently prefer
     the usage question, because duplicates can be flagged without an answer.

     ---

     3. Draft PLAN.md (half a page; this gets committed verbatim)

     ▎ Goal. Turn "which diabetic patients are overdue for follow-up" into a
     ▎ call list the front desk can trust. Every name on it comes with a reason.
     ▎ Anything uncertain goes to a separate list for a person to check.
     ▎
     ▎ First, find out (before building):
     ▎ 1. Profile each CSV: row counts, duplicates, date and unit formats,
     ▎ orphan IDs (visit_ids in prescriptions with no visit; MRNs in visits/labs
     ▎ with no patient).
     ▎ 2. How diabetes is recorded: note phrases, drugs, lab names and units.
     ▎ Who looks diabetic on one signal but not another?
     ▎ 3. How follow-up intent is recorded: the next_appointment column vs.
     ▎ intervals written in notes.
     ▎ 4. Who must never be called: deceased, transferred, duplicate records,
     ▎ missing or shared phones.
     ▎
     ▎ Working definitions (provisional; the email may change them):
     ▎ - Diabetic = a clinician documented diabetes (Type 1 or 2) in a visit
     ▎ note, or the patient is on a diabetes-only drug (not metformin alone).
     ▎ Gestational diabetes, prediabetes and metformin-for-PCOS are excluded.
     ▎ Lab-only evidence goes to doctor review, not the call list.
     ▎ - Overdue = as of the run date, no diabetes-related visit since the
     ▎ follow-up date the doctor set. That date comes from next_appointment,
     ▎ else from the interval in the last note, else a default interval (from
     ▎ guidelines or the hospital's answer).
     ▎
     ▎ Build: one Python script that reads the export and writes (a) a call list
     ▎ sorted by days overdue, with phone, last visit, due date and the
     ▎ evidence for inclusion, (b) a needs-review list, and (c) a count of who
     ▎ was excluded and why.
     ▎
     ▎ Unsure about: whether non-diabetes visits reset the clock; what default
     ▎ interval to use; whether the export includes future bookings; whether
     ▎ Type 1 children belong on the same list; how to treat records I suspect
     ▎ are the same person.
     ▎
     ▎ Check by: hand-tracing about 10 patients end-to-end, including every trap
     ▎ case above.

     (It's about 280 words. "Half a page" is satisfied.)

     ---

     4. Decisions

     Resolved (iteration 1):
     - Stack: Python + pandas, with requirements.txt. Running it takes two
       commands: install, then python overdue.py.
     - Output: call_list.csv, needs_review.csv and exclusions.csv, plus one
       plain printable HTML page showing the reason for each name.
     - Duplicate MRNs: flag, don't merge. Both records go to needs-review with
       the reason.
     - Email Q5: the usage question (one-off or weekly, print or spreadsheet,
       call outcomes).

     Still open (my defaults; change them if you disagree):
     - How specific should PLAN.md be? My default is to keep it as
       questions-to-investigate and name the trap categories generically. The
       specific findings go into DECISIONS.md after profiling confirms them with
       counts.
     - When to send the email: right after the PLAN.md commit. We keep working
       with the provisional definitions in the meantime.

     5. Execution outline (after PLAN.md is committed; detail comes later)

     1. git init in the take-home folder and commit the untouched inputs plus
        PLAN.md. Send the email.
     2. Write a profiling script and commit its output. Everything in section 1
        gets confirmed or corrected with counts.
     3. Write the normalisation layer: dates, lab names and units, drug names.
        Every transform is logged.
     4. Classify patients as diabetic / review / excluded, with an evidence
        string.
     5. Compute due dates and overdue status as of --as-of (default: today).
     6. Outputs plus hand-trace checks. Then DECISIONS.md, reply.txt and a
        README with run instructions.
     7. Commit after each step. Export the transcripts.

     6. Verification

     - Run python overdue.py --as-of 2026-10-08 from a clean clone.
     - Hand-trace the trap patients:
       - the deceased and transferred patients are absent
       - the GDM, PCOS and prediabetes patients are absent or in review
       - the duplicate pairs are flagged
       - the T1D children are handled per the decision
       - the lab-only patients are in review
     - Spot-check 5 random call-list rows against the raw CSVs.
     - Check counts reconcile: total patients = call list + not overdue + review
       + excluded.

● You rejected the write, so I haven't created PLAN.md in the folder. Here's the

  Written for: the mera.health reviewers reading your submission.

  # PLAN

  **Goal.** Turn "which diabetic patients are overdue for follow-up" into a call
  list the front desk can trust. Every name on it comes with a reason. Anything
  uncertain goes to a separate list for a person to check.

  **First, find out (before building):**
  1. Profile each CSV: row counts, duplicates, date and unit formats, orphan IDs
  (visit_ids in prescriptions with no visit; MRNs in visits/labs with no
  patient).
  2. How diabetes is recorded: note phrases, drugs, lab names and units. Who
  looks diabetic on one signal but not another?
  3. How follow-up intent is recorded: the `next_appointment` column vs.
  intervals written in notes.
  4. Who must never be called: deceased, transferred, duplicate records, missing
  or shared phones.

  **Working definitions (provisional; the email may change them):**
  - *Diabetic* = a clinician documented diabetes (Type 1 or 2) in a visit note,
  or the patient is on a diabetes-only drug (not metformin alone). Gestational
  diabetes, prediabetes and metformin-for-PCOS are excluded. Lab-only evidence
  goes to doctor review, not the call list.
  - *Overdue* = as of the run date, no diabetes-related visit since the
  follow-up date the doctor set. That date comes from `next_appointment`, else
  from the interval in the last note, else a default interval (from guidelines
  or the hospital's answer).

  **Build:** one Python script that reads the export and writes (a) a call list
  sorted by days overdue, with phone, last visit, due date and the evidence for
  inclusion, (b) a needs-review list, and (c) a count of who was excluded and
  why.

  **Unsure about:** whether non-diabetes visits reset the clock; what default
  interval to use; whether the export includes future bookings; whether Type 1
  children belong on the same list; how to treat records I suspect are the same
  person.

  **Check by:** hand-tracing about 10 patients end-to-end, including every trap
  case above.

  The email to send (5 questions):

  ▎ Hi Meenakshi,
  ▎
  ▎ Thanks for the export. A few questions before we finalise the list:
  ▎
  ▎ 1. Who should count as diabetic? Only patients a doctor has recorded as
  ▎ diabetic? Or should we also flag patients whose blood tests are in the
  ▎ diabetic range but have no diagnosis recorded? Those would go to a doctor to
  ▎ review, not straight onto the call list. Should children with Type 1
  ▎ diabetes, women who had diabetes only during pregnancy, and patients marked
  ▎ "prediabetes" be included?
  ▎ 2. What does "overdue" mean to your doctors? Is it past the review date the
  ▎ doctor wrote at the last visit, or past a fixed gap (e.g. 3 months) since
  ▎ the last diabetes check? How many days late before the front desk should
  ▎ call?
  ▎ 3. What counts as a follow-up? Does any visit count (e.g. a dental or skin
  ▎ visit), or only one where their diabetes was reviewed? Does a blood test on
  ▎ its own count?
  ▎ 4. Is the export complete? What dates and departments does it cover? Are
  ▎ appointments already booked for the future included? If not, we may list
  ▎ people who already have a booking.
  ▎ 5. How will the front desk use the list? Once, or re-run every week with a
  ▎ fresh export? Printed or in a spreadsheet? Should they be able to record
  ▎ what happened on each call?
  ▎
  ▎ Thanks,
  ▎ [your name]

  Before the first commit: this folder is inside a git repo that covers your
  whole home directory (C:\Users\Abhay JK). You need to run git init inside the
  take-home folder first, or your commit will land in that home-directory repo.
  The brief also wants PLAN.md committed before any code, so the order is: git
  init, then commit PLAN.md together with the untouched brief, email and export,
  then send the email.

  Should I write PLAN.md and run git init and that first commit, or do you want
  to change something first?

● The profiler and its report are committed (2ac357b). The report is at
  profiling/profile_report.txt.

  Confirmed from the first read: 3 deaths, 3 transfers, 4 same-person pairs
  under two MRNs, 3 families sharing a phone, 8 missing phones, the impossible
  date of birth, mixed lab names, units and dates, and 4 duplicate lab rows. Lab
  slash dates are definitely DD/MM: read as MM/DD, 238 of them don't parse.

  New, and they change the build:

  1. Matching "diabetes" in notes wrongly includes 8 people. The words appear in
     "No h/o diabetes", "Denies diabetes", "Non-diabetic", "Father diabetic"
     and "family history of diabetes". The classifier has to handle negation and
     family history, or these 8 land on the call list.
  2. My PLAN definition was wrong on one point. It counted any non-metformin
     diabetes drug as evidence, but one gestational-diabetes patient (SVH028326)
     was given insulin during pregnancy. Drug evidence from pregnancy visits
     has to be ignored. Metformin-only patients without a diabetes note all turn
     out to be PCOS or gestational, so that part of the rule holds. PLAN.md
     stays as committed; this goes in DECISIONS.md.
  3. About 8 patients have clearly diabetic blood results that nobody acted on.
     Examples: Md. Rafiq HbA1c 8.2% ("increased thirst… review with reports");
     Tejas Iyengar 8.1% (polyuria); Gowramma Angadi 8.1% ("reports to be
     collected, review SOS"); Subramanian Kumar 8.2% and 7.8%. Each was told to
     come back with reports and there's no later diabetes visit. They aren't on
     the "diabetic" list by our definition, but they may be the most important
     finding for the doctors. A few others had a borderline 6.5–6.6% that the
     doctor chose to manage with diet; those are a different case.
  4. Duplicates split diabetes histories. For Joseph D'Souza and Shivakumar, the
     diabetes notes are on one MRN and the newer prescriptions on the other. If
     we just flag both records and keep them off the call list, they also
     disappear from the diabetic list. The review list should show the pair
     together, with their combined last visit.
  5. Year typos, all confirmed by where the row sits in the file:
     - one visit dated 2015, really 2025
     - two lab rows dated a year in the future, really 2025 and 2026

     If left uncorrected, the future-dated lab would make one diabetic patient
     look recently seen.
  6. Follow-up intent: 173 visits mention diabetes. 54 have both a written
     interval and a next_appointment, 38 an interval only, 35 a date only, and
     46 neither. The most common interval written is "review 3 months".

  Before I write the classifier, I need your call on these:

● User answered Claude's questions:
  ⎿  · Where should patients with diabetic-range blood results that nobody acted
     on go (HbA1c ≥6.5%, no diabetes diagnosis in notes)? → Doctor-review list,
     highlighted (Recommended)
     · When a patient has no review date from the doctor, what default gap
     counts as overdue? → 3 months + 14-day grace (Recommended)
     · Which visits reset the follow-up clock? → Diabetes-related visits only
     (Recommended)
     · How should the year-typo dates be handled (one visit in 2015, two labs a
     year in the future)? → Correct and log (Recommended)

  On that last point, one catch before writing reply.txt. I just noticed a bug
  of mine that only shows up on older pandas. overdue.py turns blank
  next_appointment values into missing values with replace("", None). On pandas
  2.x, that call copies the previous row's date into each blank. It would
  silently give hundreds of visits a false booked date. It works on my pandas 3,
  but requirements.txt allows 2.2, which the hospital's laptop could have.
  Fixing it now:

      15  **"Diabetic"** means a doctor recorded diabetes (Type 1 or 2) i
          n a visit note, or the patient is on a diabetes medicine other 
          than metformin alone. Pregnancy visits don't count for either.
      16  - *Why not metformin:* every patient on metformin alone without
           a diabetes note has PCOS (4) or gestational diabetes (1).
      25 -- *Notes are read clause by clause.* Clauses that negate diabet
         -es or describe family history are dropped. Without this, 8 peop
         -le would have been included because of phrases like "No h/o dia
         -betes", "Denies diabetes", "Non-diabetic" and "Father diabetic"
         -.

         +'t count. Neither does a blood test on its own.                
      27  
      28  **"Overdue"** means more than 14 days past the due date, with n
          o diabetes review since. The due date comes from the last revie
          w, in this order:
      29  1. the doctor's `next_appointment`
      37 -2. else the interval written in the note ("review 3 months", "r
         -/v 3/12" = 3 months)                                           
      38 -3. else 3 months                                               
      30 +2. else the interval written in the note ("r/v 3/12" = 3 months
         +)                                                              
      31 +3. else 3 months (ADA: check every 3 months when not at goal)  
      32  
      40 -The 3-month default is the interval doctors write most often he
         -re, and it matches ADA's quarterly testing when not at goal. Th
         -e 14-day grace keeps people a few days late off the list.      
      33 +A booked date wins over the note, because the patient holds tha
         +t booking. Where the two disagree by more than a month, the rea
         +son shows both. There are 2 such cases, both NOT_DUE: Rashmi G 
         +Menon (note says May, booked 15 Oct) and U. Harish (note says J
         +une, booked 7 Oct).

      66  ## 4. What this can't be trusted for
      68 -- **Completeness.** It only sees this export (last visit 29 Sep
         - 2026). Bookings in other systems and later visits are invisibl
         -e, so someone already booked may be called.                    
      67 +- **Completeness.** Visits start in Oct 2023, so a diabetic las
         +t seen before then is not in this export at all, even though th
         +ey would be the most overdue. Bookings made at a visit are incl
         +uded (27 fall after 29 Sep). Bookings made any other way, and a
         +nything after 29 Sep, are invisible.                           
      68  - **Deaths and transfers not written in a note.** Those patient
          s will be called.
      69  - **Clinical priority.** Ordered by days overdue, not by how un
          well anyone is.
      70  - **Eye, foot and kidney checks.** "Fundus check due" and simil
          ar are not tracked.
      72 -- **The 3-month default and 14-day grace.** Both are assumption
         -s until the hospital answers.                                  
      71 +- **The edges of the list.** 6 of the 38 depend on the as-of da
         +te, default gap or grace period (§1).

         -mg/dL, repeat to confirm): https://www.aafp.org/afp/2010/0715/p
         -206 ; https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC2797382/        
      76 -- ADA HbA1c frequency (twice yearly at goal, quarterly if not):
         - https://www.aafp.org/pubs/afp/issues/2006/0901/p871.html

         + ≥ 6.5%, FPG ≥ 126 mg/dL; Rec 2.1b: confirm with a second abnor
         +mal result). *Diabetes Care* 49(Suppl 1):S27–S49: https://pmc.n
         +cbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC12690183/

         +nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC12690178/                              
      76  - HbA1c unit conversion: https://ngsp.org/ifccngsp.asp
      77  - Not looked up (agent's knowledge of Indian clinical shorthand
          ): k/c/o = known case of; r/v 3/12 = review in 3 months; OHA = 
          oral hypoglycaemic agent; GRBS = random blood sugar; metformin 
          is used for PCOS.

      26  **"Follow-up"** means a diabetes review: a diabetes or sugar di
          scussion in Diabetology, General Medicine or Paediatrics, or di
          abetes medicine prescribed. A dental or orthopaedic visit doesn
          't count. Neither does a blood test on its own.

         +nce. The due date comes from the last review, in this order:   
      29  1. the doctor's `next_appointment`
      30  2. else the interval written in the note ("r/v 3/12" = 3 months
          )
      31  3. else 3 months (ADA: check every 3 months when not at goal)

      21  ## Working definitions (details and reasons in DECISIONS.md §1)
      22  - **Diabetic:** a diabetes mention in a note clause (negations 
          and family history removed; prediabetes and gestational clauses
           excluded), or a non-metformin glucose-lowering drug. Evidence 
          from pregnancy visits is ignored.
      23  - **Follow-up visit:** diabetes or sugar content in Diabetology
          , General Medicine or Paediatrics, or a glucose-lowering drug p
          rescribed. A lab test alone doesn't count.
      24 -- **Overdue:** more than 14 days past the due date. The due dat
         -e comes from `next_appointment`, else the interval written in t
         -he note, else 3 months.                                        
      24 +- **Overdue:** more than 14 days past the due date, measured by
         + default as of the export's last visit date. The due date comes
         + from `next_appointment` (shown alongside the note when they di
         +sagree), else the interval written in the note, else 3 months. 
      25 +- **Borderline:** a CALL row that wouldn't survive a 6-month de
         +fault, a 30-day grace, or measuring from the export date gets "
         +check the appointment book first". It must match `check_assumpt
         +ions.py`.                                                      
      26  - These are working assumptions until the hospital answers the 
          5-question email (see DECISIONS §0). If they answer, update the
           constants at the top of `overdue.py` and DECISIONS.

  - The tool now measures "overdue" as of the export's last date, 29 September,
    unless you give it another date. On that basis 42 diabetics are overdue, and

  I added two things you didn't ask for, because the dropdown is useless without
  them:
  - Choices are remembered. On a page opened straight from disk, a choice would
    otherwise be lost on reload. They're stored in that browser on that
    computer, separately for each list date, so last week's "no answer" doesn't
    carry onto a new list. Nothing is sent anywhere.

    can hand to the records team.

  to write. The button and instructions are hidden when printed.

      146  say("Diabetes-mention visits with neither an interval phrase n
           or next_appointment (sample):")
      147  show(visits[diab_visit & ~has_interval & ~has_nappt], ["visit_
           id", "mrn", "visit_date", "notes"], max_rows=25)
      148  
      149 +# Is next_appointment a booking the patient keeps? For dates t
          +hat fell due by the end of the                                
      150 +# export, count those with any other visit by the same patient
          + near that date. (Raw dates; the                              
      151 +# one year typo in visits.csv has no next_appointment and its 
          +patient has none nearby.)                                     
      152 +export_end = vdate.max()                                      
      153 +gap = pd.Timedelta(days=14)                                   
      154 +due_by_end = visits[nappt.notna() & (nappt <= export_end)]    
      155 +by_mrn = pd.DataFrame({"mrn": visits["mrn"], "visit_id": visit
          +s["visit_id"], "d": vdate}).groupby("mrn")                    
      156 +                                                              
      157 +                                                              
      158 +def attended(row, inclusive=True, after_only=False):          
      159 +    others = by_mrn.get_group(row["mrn"])                     
      160 +    days = (others.loc[others["visit_id"] != row["visit_id"], 
          +"d"] - nappt[row.name]).dt.days                               
      161 +    near = (days.abs() <= gap.days) if inclusive else (days.ab
          +s() < gap.days)                                               
      162 +    return bool((near & (days >= 0) if after_only else near).a
          +ny())                                                         
      163 +                                                              
      164 +                                                              
      165 +n = len(due_by_end)                                           
      166 +within = due_by_end.apply(attended, axis=1).sum()             
      167 +strict = due_by_end.apply(attended, axis=1, inclusive=False).s
          +um()                                                          
      168 +after = due_by_end.apply(attended, axis=1, after_only=True).su
          +m()                                                           
      169 +say(f"next_appointment dates due by the export end ({export_en
          +d.date()}): {n}")                                             
      170 +say(f"  followed by a visit within 14 days either side of the 
          +date: {within} of {n}"                                        
      171 +    f"  (strictly under 14 days: {strict}; on or after the dat
          +e, within 14 days: {after})")                                 
      172 +                                                              
      173  # ------------------------------------------------------------
           ---------------
      174  section("6. LAB RESULTS")
      175  say("Lab rows whose MRN is not in patients.csv:")

       5 -## 0. Decisions made before writing code                       
       6 -- **Ask only what changes the build.** The email to the hospita
         -l asks: who counts as diabetic, what "overdue" means, which vis
         -its count as follow-up, whether the export is complete, and how
         - the list will be used. I didn't ask about things I could work 
         -out myself (formats, units, thresholds, drug classes, duplicate
         -s). No reply yet; §1 lists the working assumptions.            
       7 -- **Profile before building** (`profile_export.py`, read-only).
         - Raw export committed untouched; all cleaning is in code and lo
         -gged.                                                          
       8 -- **Flag, don't merge, suspected duplicates.** A wrong merge se
         -nds a real call to the wrong person.                           
       9 -- **Three lists** (call / needs review / excluded), so uncertai
         -n cases go to a person. Output is CSVs for Excel plus one print
         -able HTML page, run with two commands. `output/` is not committ
         -ed: it holds names and phones.                                 
      10 -- **Measure from the export's last date, not today** (decided a
         -fter the first version). The same export then always gives the 
         -same list, whenever it's run. Measuring to today, 8 Oct, would 
         -add 3 people who may well have visited after the export was tak
         -en. `--as-of` still allows any date, and with a fresh weekly ex
         -port the two dates are nearly the same.                        
      11 -- **Review list split by who acts on it** (decided after the fi
         -rst version): one list for a doctor, one for the records team. 
         -A double-registered person is shown once. `all_patients.csv` st
         -ill has one row per hospital number.                           
      12 -- **Where PLAN.md was wrong** (it stays as committed): it count
         -ed any non-metformin diabetes drug as proof of diabetes. One ge
         -stational-diabetes patient was given insulin in pregnancy, so d
         -rugs prescribed at pregnancy visits are ignored.               
       5 +## 0. Approach                                                 
       6 +- **Ask the hospital only what changes the build:** who is diab
         +etic, what "overdue" means, what counts as follow-up, whether t
         +he export is complete, and how the list will be used. No reply 
         +yet, so §1 holds the working assumptions.                      
       7 +- **Profile before building.** The raw export is committed unto
         +uched; all cleaning is in code and logged.                     
       8 +- **Flag suspected duplicates, never merge them.** A wrong merg
         +e means calling the wrong person.                              
       9 +- **Uncertain cases go to a person.** There are separate lists 
         +to call, for a doctor, for the records team, and excluded.     
      10 +- **Measure from the export's last date, not today,** so the sa
         +me export always gives the same list. `--as-of` overrides this.
      11 +- **Where PLAN.md was wrong** (it stays as committed): it took 
         +any non-metformin diabetes drug as proof of diabetes. A gestati
         +onal-diabetes patient was given insulin, so drugs prescribed at
         + pregnancy visits are ignored.

      13  ## 1. Terms in the email
      15 -**"Diabetic"** means a doctor recorded diabetes (Type 1 or 2) i
         -n a visit note, or the patient is on a diabetes medicine other 
         -than metformin alone. Pregnancy visits don't count for either. 
         -Children with Type 1 diabetes stay on the same list (PLAN.md's 
         -open question), with a caller note to speak to a parent or guar
         -dian.                                                          
      16 -- *Why not metformin:* every patient on metformin alone without
         - a diabetes note has PCOS (4) or gestational diabetes (1).     
      17 -- *Notes are read clause by clause.* Clauses that negate diabet
         -es or describe family history are dropped. Without this, 8 peop
         -le would have been included because of phrases like "No h/o dia
         -betes", "Denies diabetes" and "Father diabetic".               
      18 -- *Excluded:*                                                  
      19 -  - gestational diabetes only (2)                              
      20 -  - prediabetes / borderline sugars (4)                        
      21 -  - one diabetic-range HbA1c that was normal on repeat (3). ADA
         - requires two abnormal results.                                
      22 -- *Caught in review of the first version:* "Impaired fasting gl
         -ucose" written out in full wasn't recognised as prediabetes, on
         -ly the abbreviation "IFG". It's fixed, and that patient moved f
         -rom NOT_DIABETIC to EXCLUDED.                                  
      23 -- *Wrongly left out:* diabetics whose notes use none of our wor
         -ds and who take only metformin; anyone diagnosed elsewhere.    
      24 -- *Wrongly included:* a note like "?DM" (query diabetes) would 
         -count. I saw none, but the check is a word match, not a reading
         -.

         -s sent anywhere. The list of outcomes is provisional until the

         -review SOS"; 6.6% after gestational diabetes. A diagnosis needs
         - a doctor (and, per ADA, a second abnormal result). Each row li
         -sts every diabetic-range result across the person's records. 4

      57  ## 4. What this can't be trusted for
      64 -- **Completeness.** Every patient in the export has a visit on 
         -or after 2 Oct 2023, so a diabetic last seen before then is not
         - in this export at all, even though they would be the most over
         -due. Next-appointment dates set at a visit are included (27 fal
         -l after 29 Sep). Bookings made any other way, and anything afte
         -r 29 Sep, are invisible.                                       
      65 -- **Whether a next-appointment date was kept or confirmed.** Th
         -e field is the date the doctor set. Of the 206 such dates due b
         -y 29 Sep, only 18 had any visit by that patient within 14 days 
         -either side of the date (6 on or after it; `profile_export.py`)
         -. So a date in the future means "the doctor wanted them back th
         -en", not "they're booked and will come".                       
      58 +- **Completeness.** Every patient in the export has a visit on 
         +or after 2 Oct 2023. Diabetics last seen before then, who would
         + be the most overdue, aren't in the export at all. Anything aft
         +er 29 Sep is invisible.                                        
      59 +- **Appointment dates.** `next_appointment` is the date the doc
         +tor set. Of the 206 such dates due by 29 Sep, only 17 were foll
         +owed by a visit within 14 days (6 on or after the date; `profil
         +e_export.py`). 27 more fall after 29 Sep. A future date means "
         +the doctor wanted them back then", not "they're booked".       
      60  - **Deaths and transfers not written in a note.** Those patient
          s will be called.
      67 -- **Clinical priority.** Ordered by days overdue, not by how un
         -well anyone is.                                                
      68 -- **Eye, foot and kidney checks.** "Fundus check due" and simil
         -ar are not tracked.                                            
      69 -- **The edges of the list.** 4 of the 35 depend on the default 
         -gap or grace period, and they're marked borderline (§1).       
      61 +- **Clinical priority.** The list is ordered by days overdue, n
         +ot by how unwell anyone is.                                    
      62 +- **Eye, foot and kidney checks.** These aren't tracked.       
      63 +- **The edges of the list.** 4 of the 35 depend on assumptions 
         +and are marked borderline.

      78 -## 6. What I'd do next                                         
      79 -1. **Apply the hospital's answers** when they arrive: the defin
         -itions, `--default-months` and `--grace-days`, then re-run and 
         -update every figure.                                           
      80 -2. **Check the appointment book.** Matching the call list again
         -st the real booking system would remove the biggest wrong-call 
         -risk (§4).                                                     
      81 -3. **A merge workflow for duplicate records,** confirmed by a p
         -erson, so merged patients go back into the normal flow.        
      82 -4. **Record call outcomes,** once the hospital says how they'll
         - use the list (question 5). The Outcome column is a paper stand
         --in.                                                           
      83 -5. **Reach patients last seen before Oct 2023** from an older e
         -xport.                                                         
      84 -6. **Let doctors set priority.** Ordering by clinical risk (e.g
         -. HbA1c) is a clinical judgement; I'd offer it as an option for
         - doctors to choose, not a default.                             
      85 -7. **Eye, foot and kidney checks** ("fundus check due", "urine 
         -microalbumin adv") as separate overdue items.                  
      86 -8. **A small test set for the note reader:** the phrases that f
         -ooled it (negations, "impaired fasting glucose"), so later chan
         -ges can't bring those mistakes back.

          ion).

● The attendance count is 17 of 206, my earlier explanation for 18 was wrong,

  1. Attendance count. Printing the 18 rows found the problem:
  - The wrong match: SVH029235's appointment was set by visit V042725 on 22 Sep
    2025, and the "match" was V042723, a different visit by that patient on the
    same day. It isn't the setting visit, so my check let it through, but it
    can't count as attending the appointment. That one row is exactly 14 days
    from the appointment date, which is why my "day 14" theory happened to
    give 17. The real rule is that a match must come after the visit that set
    the date, and that gives 17.
  - The 2015 date: it changes nothing. Raw and fixed dates give the same counts,
    because that patient has no appointment dates.
  - 4 vs 6: 6 appointments had some visit on or after the date within 14 days.
    Two of them (SVH028721, SVH029528) also had a visit a few days before the
    date, and that earlier visit is the nearer one. Judged by each appointment's
    nearest visit, you get your 4.
  - What I used: 17, with 6 "on or after", in DECISIONS.md and the code comment.
    The profiler prints all three numbers (17, 6 and 4).

  2. "Booked" wording. DECISIONS.md now says "date set for 15 Oct" and "date set
  for 7 Oct". The tool's reason text says "appointment date used". In
  all_patients.csv, only those two reason strings changed (SVH027094 and
  SVH027782).
